# B0244 — Visibility, access and the costs of a useful result

These three Event families examine what it takes to make a person visible without treating that visibility as full access. A magazine feature draws meaning from ordinary routines while leaving privacy and public reach negotiable. NI+CORA turns a work deadline into a challenge about personal presentation, then repairs some of the resulting harm without making the provocation necessary. A four-person hip-hop project distributes teaching, care and coordination, yet its reported success leaves Ei unable to remember a process conducted through her body. The comparison is formal and ethical; the catalog gives no common calendar or cross-family cause.

#### Interpretation and bounded alternatives

**The chapter makes participation a set of distinct negotiations rather than one shared enthusiasm.** Miu's interest, Honoka's request for prior consultation and Murasaki's qualified assistance are separately spoken at P17-P20. A summary that merely says all three are eager would remove consequential differences in their conditions and stated motives.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=7#/Pages/7`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=8#/Pages/8`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=9#/Pages/9`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=10#/Pages/10`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=17#/Pages/17`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=18#/Pages/18`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=19#/Pages/19`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=20#/Pages/20`.

**Privacy is acknowledged as a limit on managerial compulsion, while cooperation remains socially requested.** Player's P10 refusal to force participation is explicit. Murasaki's P20 reference to not troubling him gives her assistance a relational cost, without establishing coercion, romance, literal kinship or an unconditional commitment.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=8#/Pages/8`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=9#/Pages/9`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0123.json__e4da279669d7f153?doc=primary&log=10#/Pages/10`.

#### Interpretation and bounded alternatives

**The narrative revises what counts as after-school time without removing the obligation that limits it.** P1-P2 initially negate leisure through family work. P14-P16 find a valued interval for care, and P20 closes it at opening time. The change is a new use of constrained time, not an escape from work or proof that her workload is solved.

### Whole-family reconstruction, knowledge and literary synthesis

#### Factual reconstruction

- **Chapter:** 503012301

**Reconstruction:** Player proposes a magazine feature on after-school lives and explains its format and privacy limit. Miu agrees through interest, Honoka requests prior staff consultation, and Murasaki offers qualified help. Content remains for the participants to consider.
- **Chapter:** 503012302

**Reconstruction:** Honoka presents work scheduling, strict but valued tea practice, elective sweets procurement and comfort in familiar hometown confectionery. Her ordinary life acquires specificity, yet she still wonders whether the account is article-worthy.
- **Chapter:** 503012303

**Reconstruction:** Miu adds lacrosse to her account of shopping with friends. A friend's one-time plea and an appealing uniform led to trying the activity; movement, shared outcomes and conversation afterward became further pleasures. She invites a future uniform photo request.
- **Chapter:** 503012304

**Reconstruction:** Murasaki narrates rescuing a cat on an unusual rainy-day route. Care, riverbank walks and vocal practice give value to time still constrained by shop work. She suggests an unspecified resemblance and refuses to elaborate; the cat's undemanding company feels comfortable.
- **Chapter:** 503012305

**Reconstruction:** Player reports reading the three accounts, and discussion yields compliments, surprise and requests to see more. Murasaki challenges attention to Honoka and refuses a cat visit on a hypothetical allergy ground, which Player accepts. The group ends with encouragement about qualitative discovery, without measured public results.

#### Causal structure

**Family spine:** A proposed public feature creates a need to select ordinary life for representation; three distinct self-accounts answer it; internal reception discovers differences and tests the boundaries of further access; the ending offers qualitative value while leaving commercial benefit uncertain.

**Distinct chapter causes:** - Honoka's work schedule permits a club routine; the club's purchasing rotation creates elective opportunities for taste, skill and familiar comfort.
- A peer's short-term need and Miu's aesthetic interest initiate an experiment whose enjoyable physical and social experiences help explain continuation.
- A route change produces Murasaki's encounter with a vulnerable animal; refusing abandonment gives rise to a constrained but valued care routine.
- Player's curiosity about the revealed routines prompts future offers and a refusal; the refusal produces a local acknowledgment of responsibility.

**Noncauses:** The catalog does not cause these events or locate them relative to prior Event families. Earlier character histories are rival contexts, not automatic causes. Player does not create all three offstage lives through this feature.

**Uncompleted links:** No shown staff consultation, editorial production sequence, commissioned lacrosse shoot, cat visit, completed allergy check or independently observed fan acquisition fills the gaps between invitation, accounts and closing encouragement.

#### Epistemic boundaries

- The reader receives three first-person accounts, including selected memories. Those words establish what Honoka, Miu and Murasaki report; they are not independent verification of every offstage event.
- Player explicitly says he has read all three by 503012305/P1. Murasaki's response to Honoka, Honoka's interest in the uniform and Miu's interest in the cat establish particular exchanges; no omniscient ledger of what every participant read or knows is inferred.
- Honoka's consultation request in chapter1 is not shown fulfilled or denied. The later existence of readable accounts does not authorize fabricating the missing meeting.
- Murasaki's analogy to the cat is deliberately underexplained. The older admitted stray-cat metaphor belongs to her, whereas the chapter4 animal is literal and locally unnamed; neither supplies a complete biography for the other.
- The possible cat allergy is hypothetical. No current allergy holder is identified and no medical fact is inferred about a named character.
- Murasaki predicts a limited public response and Player expresses confidence in qualitative recognition. No quoted audience response, count or independently verified publication date is supplied.
- Offers to bring the uniform and to be more careful remain promises, distinct from completed acts. The group reply does not prove identical internal feelings.
- All five case freezes actually precede this family freeze and every next-family exposure. The boundary is a real execution record, not a reconstructed clean timestamp.

#### Character relations

- **Subjects:** Honoka and Player

**Relation:** The invitation permits ordinary self-presentation, yet Honoka continues to seek reassurance and downplay her worth. Player's interest in her serious club practice is stated; her uncertainty is not cured by his encouragement.
- **Subjects:** Miu and Player

**Relation:** Miu seeks his response to her account, receives specific surprise and recognition, and offers to bring the uniform. This is a bounded work/social exchange, not proof of exclusivity, romance or unlimited access.
- **Subjects:** Murasaki toward Honoka and Player

**Relation:** Murasaki compliments Honoka and also intervenes when she characterizes Player's attention as teasing. His denied intention and her intervention remain separately attributed.
- **Subjects:** Murasaki toward the cat

**Relation:** She chooses care and values company that does not continually demand appraisal of her serious practice. The animal's private intention remains inaccessible and shop obligations continue.
- **Subjects:** Murasaki toward the Nanasta group

**Relation:** The cat refusal extends attention beyond the immediate people who want to see it, using potential effects on others to limit access. Player accepts this local correction; no permanent institutional reform is proven.
- **Subjects:** Honoka and the tea club; Miu and lacrosse friends

**Relation:** Rotating procurement and recurring conversation locate the idols in peer routines beyond Nanasta. The unnamed peers do not become invented named identities or a complete school census.

**Ordinariness becomes narratively informative before its public market value is settled.** The everyday practices in chapters2-4 specify disciplined taste, unexpected sport and constrained care. Chapter5 offers internal recognition but only a tentative account of fans.

#### Interpretation and bounded alternatives

**The disagreement changes from the use of rehearsal time to the standard by which a unit member's contribution is judged.** Musubi's P13/P21 criterion is protecting practice for an imminent release. Susu's P24-P28 answer makes greater sexiness and male response conditions for growth, then casts Musubi's guardedness as a unit obstacle. That asserted criterion remains Susu's argument, not an established measure of artistic development.

#### Interpretation and bounded alternatives

**The challenge generates a cost that its simple proof-of-contribution frame failed to accommodate.** Musubi's attempt produces explicit objections, an interruption, conflict over the rules, and her later wish to leave. Player's final private assessment concerns disrupted rehearsal, not a completed artistic advance.

**Praise can fail when it repeats the criterion that now embarrasses its recipient.** At P48-P49 Player tries to reassure Musubi by praising the contested performance, and she asks him not to say it. His intention and her response differ; a compliment does not by itself establish restored comfort.

#### Causal structure

An accepted competitive challenge escalates beyond the calm comparison it promised. Objections and Susu's intervention convert the attempt into a dispute over boundaries and attention; public self-reassessment then produces Musubi's regret and attempted withdrawal. Reassurance repeats an unwelcome criterion and fails locally, while the original rehearsal goal remains displaced.

The disruptive challenge is followed by an acknowledgment of lost composure, explanation, specific praise and a debate over why the challenge was needed. Musubi locates some responsibility in Susu's provoking behavior; Susu offers limited care and admits her own jealousy. Qualified agreement permits a declared return to work, while Player privately interprets the duo as compatible and sister-like.

#### Retained original questions and limits

- The apology and small promise permit a local repair while earlier pressure, regret and objections remain historical evidence.
- Player's sister comparison is a private analogy and compatibility question, not literal kinship or omniscient certification.

### Whole-family reconstruction, knowledge and literary synthesis

#### Factual reconstruction

- **Chapter:** 503012801

**Reconstruction:** A flirtatious practice exchange prompts Musubi's objection about rehearsal urgency. Susu attributes jealousy, which Musubi denies, then claims Musubi's guardedness obstructs unit growth. Musubi knowingly accepts a provocative challenge to disprove that charge; Player privately worries about the new song.
- **Chapter:** 503012802

**Reconstruction:** Susu tells Player she wants fans to see Musubi's range beyond anger; he privately considers Susu a cause of that anger. Musubi sets a one-day limit and aims to be judged cuter than Susu. Her adaptation of darling to anata is received by Susu as an unexpected newlywed role.
- **Chapter:** 503012803

**Reconstruction:** The challenge escalates, Player objects, and Susu interrupts. The pair disputes the rules and each other's motives. Musubi later regrets taking up the provocation and says she will leave; Player's reassurance is unwelcome, while Susu requests more attention. Rehearsal is displaced rather than completed.
- **Chapter:** 503012804

**Reconstruction:** Susu apologizes for losing composure. Musubi restates her competitive explanation and hears specific praise; both receive reassurance about growth. Susu says she already believed in growth, wanted other expressions, promises a little care and explicitly admits jealousy. Qualified agreement permits a declared return to preparation; Player privately compares the pair to sisters.

#### Causal structure

**Family spine:** A work-time objection is reframed as a deficiency in unit contribution; that accusation makes Musubi's competitiveness a lever for an accepted challenge. An imitated address yields an unexpected role implication, escalation exposes incompatible limits, and the resulting regret requires explanation and a partial repair before returning to work.

**Different stakes:** Musubi explicitly wants to protect practice and refute being a hindrance; Susu asserts public-image range and later acknowledges wanting to see other expressions and feeling jealous. Player tries to redirect work, objects to escalation, seeks reassurance and finally interprets the partnership. None is simply an omniscient judge of the other two.

**Changed information:** Susu's declared prior confidence and her own jealousy become explicit only in the final chapter. They update the completed family reading without being inserted into earlier known-then freezes as established facts.

**Bounded outcome:** A local apology, modest promise and willingness to prepare are shown. A live result, a new-song rehearsal outcome, an agreed permanent performance standard and a durable boundary protocol are not.

#### Epistemic boundaries

- Musubi's denial of jealousy, Susu's accusation and Musubi's rehearsal explanation are separately attributed. Later Susu self-attribution does not logically prove Musubi's denied motive.
- Player's thoughts about Susu provoking anger, the ruined practice and sister-like compatibility are private narrator access, not common knowledge or neutral omniscient verdicts.
- Susu's final claim that she believed in growth from the start is retrospective self-report. It complicates the initial obstruction claim without proving a fully planned beneficial lesson or foreknowledge of all consequences.
- The acting-practice claim, each partner's praise and Player's growth reassurance are reported appraisals. They do not establish objectively measured performance quality or heard acting competence.
- The anata/newlywed interpretation is a word-and-context turn between intention and reception; it is neither a universally marital lexical rule nor an actual marriage.
- Claims of threatened attention, play, fidelity and possession are conversational positions in the dispute, not proof of an exclusive romantic contract or ownership.
- Musubi's extreme self-reproach supports distress and regret. No clinical diagnosis, literal self-harm plan, act or permanent recovery is supplied.
- The final agreement is qualified, and Susu promises only a little care. The narrator's familiar/sibling framing cannot erase previous objections or certify every future interaction as harmless.

#### Character relations

- **Subjects:** Musubi toward NI+CORA

**Relation:** She protects practice and reacts strongly to being called an obstacle. Competitiveness is linked to valued contribution; her knowingly accepted challenge and subsequent regret both remain part of her agency.
- **Subjects:** Susu toward Musubi

**Relation:** Susu seeks a wider visible range but uses provocation, is surprised by the response, intervenes and later apologizes for losing composure. Praise and a modest promise coexist with the costs of the method.
- **Subjects:** Musubi toward Susu

**Relation:** She imitates a form of address, disputes asymmetrical rules, attributes threatened attention and finally proposes that Susu reduce provocation instead of treating Musubi's anger as an isolated defect.
- **Subjects:** Both members toward Player

**Relation:** Player is simultaneously an audience, work coordinator and participant with objections. His reactions are contested and solicited; his comfort and boundaries are not made irrelevant by the challenge.
- **Subjects:** Susu toward Player

**Relation:** Her explicit admission of jealousy and requests for comparable attention give her earlier confident posture a new limit. This is a local stated motive, not proof of every aspect of her emotional life.
- **Subjects:** Musubi toward Player

**Relation:** She gives a competitive explanation but dislikes how simply he accepts it. The tension supports an open question about recognition without requiring a definitive hidden-romance diagnosis.
- **Subjects:** NI+CORA within its admitted history

**Relation:** Earlier vulnerability, privacy breach, reversed care, collapse and extra-training solidarity remain relevant rival contexts. The final sister analogy recurs as an attributed model, not literal kinship or a claim that dependence and pressure have disappeared.

**A demand to prove growth can distort the very work it invokes.** The approaching new song begins as Musubi's reason to practise, becomes leverage against her, and is still unfinished when Player privately calls the session disorderly. Return to preparation closes the diversion rather than proving the diversion necessary.

**Performance range and personal access are not interchangeable.** The challenge turns image into a response demanded from a particular partner, produces objections and regret, and is later re-described through acting practice. #### Interpretation and bounded alternatives

**The assignment tests a proposed relation between idol presentation and a genre-specific venue.** Coney's reported cool-and-cute claim is the ambition, while Ei's qualified recollection marks a specialized event. The aspiration does not prove existing skill, audience acceptance, formal booking or that idols universally master every genre.

#### Causal structure

A reported promotional ambition motivates a festival assignment. A roster question reveals an absent participant; the ambulance's arrival and Saori's account explain the delay. Relief permits a return to the proposed group task, ending with collective willingness before actual preparation.

### Whole-family reconstruction, knowledge and literary synthesis

#### Factual reconstruction

- **Chapter:** 503013201

**Reconstruction:** Player conveys Coney's wish to show idols making hip-hop cool and cute and assigns Ei, Sisara, Jeda and Saori to a specialized festival. Saori's delayed ambulance arrival interrupts assembly; her accident, first aid and transport are reported before the group agrees to start.
- **Chapter:** 503013202

**Reconstruction:** The Ei/Sisara pair reports a long practice interval, but Ei cannot remember teaching. Sisara praises the instruction, and changed speech leads Player to identify possession. Sisara asks to continue, and practice lasts until Ei's reported return. The teacher remains unnamed in this case's actual freeze.
- **Chapter:** 503013203

**Reconstruction:** Jeda reports some hip-hop familiarity and encourages Saori. Her care during hazards and bandaging is credited; further hazards qualify Saori's reassurance. Jeda connects the office's demanding younger-sister figures to relief from homesickness.
- **Chapter:** 503013204

**Reconstruction:** The intended ensemble rehearsal is diverted into a proposed spirit explanation for Saori's misfortune. Player corrects exorcist and fainting identifications. The recurring teacher resumes through Ei, Sisara names the lesson Jacob-style, and the group practises until Ei returns again.
- **Chapter:** 503013205

**Reconstruction:** The festival is reported successful. Ei reports missing memory and bodily unease, while Player and Jeda choose withholding and infer a beneficial final wish and departure. A reported peer invitation prompts plans for another live. Jacob directly names himself through Ei and returns, contradicting departure; concern about Ei's missing participation remains without a completed remedy.

#### Causal structure

**Project spine:** A publicity ambition creates a genre task; parallel practice develops different resources; assembled training uses recurring instruction through Ei; the event is reported successful and generates a wish to perform again.

**Parallel paths:** Sisara's learning is associated with an unresolved-then-later-named possessor in Ei, whereas Jeda supplies her own reported familiarity and practical care for Saori. These are not interchangeable capacities or equivalent states of consent.

**Interruption and repair:** Physical mishaps, genre assumptions, mistaken spiritual roles and waiting divert the work. The group repeatedly returns to practice, but no completed protocol addresses each cost.

**Counterclosure:** Jeda's report of the spirit's final enjoyment supports Player's departure inference only provisionally; Jacob's return defeats it within the final chapter.

**Bounded outcome:** The principal festival outcome is reported success, not directly inspected performance. The proposed second live/party, full disclosure to Ei, informed permission, successful cleansing and lasting retention of skill remain unshown.

#### Epistemic boundaries

- Coney's proposed cool/cute goal is quoted through Player; it is not direct access to her inner plan or independent genre research.
- Ei's early event familiarity and later bodily movement do not establish stable conscious hip-hop expertise. Her own lack of memory explicitly contests that model.
- The native Ei label and visual body remain constant while agency is separately qualified. The teacher is unnamed in503013202, named by Sisara in503013204/P29 with Player's uptake, and self-names as Jacob in503013205/P26. Both later disclosure records preserve the earlier freeze.
- Jacob's attributed American identity and embedded account of Tom, a boss and a violent past remain locally sourced claims. No independent nationality, death history, race-based aptitude or identity merge with Rei/Yuuko is established.
- Saori's accident, mild-injury and first-aid reports are not medical clearance. Recurrent help and smiling do not make injury harmless; no universal curse or immunity is derived.
- Saori's possible evil-spirit cause, Jeda's exorcist assumptions and the apparent fainting receive corrections or remain unconfirmed. Genuine diegetic possession does not validate every supernatural explanation.
- Jeda's no-harm appraisal and the group's benefit are not Ei's judgment. Player's stated protective withholding leaves her question unanswered; no consent to continued possession or secrecy is shown.
- Saori tentatively links avoiding further injury to a charm and reports Kajika's message. Neither a charm mechanism, independently fetched message, universal peer intent nor completed afterparty follows.
- Player asks about fatigue before the second-live proposal. Jeda, Sisara and Saori respond differently; Ei gives no equivalent assent as herself before Jacob returns.
- Player's departure claim is directly contradicted, and Jeda identifies him as its author. A completed release or exorcism must not enter the resulting state.

#### Character relations

- **Subjects:** Ei and the working group

**Relation:** Ei shifts from the admitted perceiver/caregiver context into a body-owner whose work is praised while her memory is absent and the explanation withheld. The group benefits, but her own experience and permission remain a distinct obligation.
- **Subjects:** Sisara toward learning and Ei

**Relation:** Sisara values exacting instruction and refuses to take the festival lightly, repeatedly encouraging continuation. Her professionalism supplies effort and a limit: useful learning does not independently settle the agency cost.
- **Subjects:** Jeda toward Saori

**Relation:** Practical intervention, bandaging and confidence in learning coexist with demands on the carer. Jeda names belonging and relief from homesickness without erasing her distant sister or the burden of care.
- **Subjects:** Jeda toward the group and Jacob

**Relation:** She moves from fascinated misclassification and camera interest to reported benefit and agreement with withholding, then corrects Player's failed departure story. These are distinct local actions, not an infallible rescuer role or a complete malicious intention.
- **Subjects:** Saori toward carers and work

**Relation:** She voices gratitude, persists, offers reassurance and joins the desire for another performance, while concrete hazards and others' interventions bound the model. Her late concern for Ei does not become an enacted remedy.
- **Subjects:** Player toward the ensemble

**Relation:** He initiates and coordinates, notices hazards, corrects labels and asks about fatigue, yet does not control the teaching and withholds relevant information from Ei. Technical success does not make every managerial choice adequate.
- **Subjects:** Jacob toward the performers

**Relation:** The later-named teacher adapts instruction to festival and party contexts through Ei's body. His reported enjoyment and continuing eagerness do not establish Ei's informed participation or an independently verified biography.
- **Subjects:** The four-member project

**Relation:** Jeda explicitly calls their arrangement a unit and wishes it to continue beyond one event. This supports a bounded project formation, without a supplied band name, permanent contract, full governance or later completed appearance.

**The project succeeds through a distributed and ethically uneven infrastructure.** Teaching, reported prior familiarity, practical first aid, protective attention, coordination and supernatural mediation all contribute. The group result and the individual costs are different objects of appraisal.
